q;           // Dividend yield\n    double r;           // Risk-free rate\n    double t;           // Time to maturity\n    double vol;         // Volatility\n    double value;       // Expected value (for validation)\n    double tol;         // Tolerance\n};\n\n// Standard normal cumulative distribution function\ninline double cumulativeNormal(const double x) noexcept {\n    return 0.5 * (1.0 + std::erf(x * M_SQRT1_2));\n}\n\n// Standard normal probability density function\ninline double normalPDF(const double x) noexcept {\n    return M_1_SQRTPI * M_SQRT1_2 * exp(-0.5 * x * x);\n}\n\n// Black-Scholes formula for European options\ndouble blackScholes(const OptionInput& option) noexcept {\n    const double S = option.spot;\n    const double K = option.strike;\n    const double r = option.r;\n    const double q = option.q;\n    const double T = option.t;\n    const double sigma = option.vol;\n    \n    if (T <= 0.0 || sigma <= 0.0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n    \n    const double d1 = (log(S / K) + (r - q + 0.5 * sigma * sigma) * T) / (sigma * sqrt(T));\n    const double d2 = d1 - sigma * sqrt(T);\n    \n    const double Nd1 = cumulativeNormal(d1);\n    const double Nd2 = cumulativeNormal(d2);\n    const double discount = exp(-r * T);\n    \n    double price;\n    if (option.type == CALL) {\n        price = S * exp(-q * T) * Nd1 - K * discount * Nd2;\n    } else { // PUT\n        price = K * discount * cumulativeNormal(-d2) - S * exp(-q * T) * cumulativeNormal(-d1);\n    }\n    \n    return price;\n}\n\n// Device-side Black-Scholes evaluation.\n__device__ __forceinline__ double cumulativeNormalDevice(const double x) noexcept {\n    return 0.5 * (1.0 + erf(x * M_SQRT1_2));\n}\n\n__device__ __forceinline__ double blackScholesDevice(const int type,\n                                                     const double K,\n                                                     const double S,\n                                                     const double q,\n                                                     const double r,\n                                                     const double T,\n                                                     const double sigma) noexcept {\n    if (T <= 0.0 || sigma <= 0.0) {\n        return 0.0;\n    }\n\n    const double sqrtT = sqrt(T);\n    const double sigmaSqrtT = sigma * sqrtT;\n    const double d1 = (log(S / K) + (r - q + 0.5 * sigma * sigma) * T) / sigmaSqrtT;\n    const double d2 = d1 - sigmaSqrtT;\n\n    const double Nd1 = cumulativeNormalDevice(d1);\n    const double Nd2 = cumulativeNormalDevice(d2);\n    const double discount = exp(-r * T);\n    const double dividendDiscount = exp(-q * T);\n\n    if (type == CALL) {\n        return S * dividendDiscount * Nd1 - K * discount * Nd2;\n    } else { // PUT\n        return K * discount * cumulativeNormalDevice(-d2) -\n               S * dividendDiscount * cumulativeNormalDevice(-d1);\n    }\n}\n\n// Structure-of-arrays kernel: coalesced loads and no unused validation fields.\n__global__ __launch_bounds__(256)\nvoid blackScholesKernel(const int* __restrict__ type,\n                        const double* __restrict__ strike,\n                        const double* __restrict__ spot,\n                        const double* __restrict__ q,\n                        const double* __restrict__ r,\n                        const double* __restrict__ t,\n                        const double* __restrict__ vol,\n                        double* __restrict__ results,\n                        const unsigned long long numOptions) {\n    const unsigned long long stride =\n        static_cast<unsigned long long>(gridDim.x) * blockDim.x;\n    for (unsigned long long i =\n             static_cast<unsigned long long>(blockIdx.x) * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n         i < numOptions;\n         i += stride) {\n        results[i] = blackScholesDevice(type[i], strike[i], spot[i], q[i],  CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&strike), n * sizeof(double)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&spot), n * sizeof(double)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&q), n * sizeof(double)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&r), n * sizeof(double)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&t), n * sizeof(double)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&vol), n * sizeof(double)));\n        CUDA_CHECK(cudaMallocHost(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&results), n * sizeof(double)));\n    }\n\n    ~HostOptions() {\n        if (type) cudaFreeHost(type);\n        if (strike) cudaFreeHost(strike);\n On -march=native (Zen/Intel with FMA), glibc's own library isn't recompiled; it's precompiled for baseline x86-64 with ifunc selecting FMA3 variants at runtime (e.g., __exp_fma). Those algorithms differ from non-FMA. Replicating exact glibc runtime behavior is complex.\n\nThus exact hash is infeasible. Accept.\n\nNow, performance optimizations: The kernel is compute-bound? 100M options in 106ms => 0.94 G options/s. RTX 3090 FP64 is low. erf/exp/log each maybe significant. So not FP64 bound; likely special function unit (SFU) for exp/erf/log? double exp is software polynomial using FP64. Hmm 0.94G options/s * 3 transcendentals ~ 2.8G transcendentals/s. Plausible.\n\nMemory bandwidth: 64 bytes/option*0.94G = 60 GB/s < 936 GB/s, not bandwidth bound. So kernel compute bound.\n\nCould we reduce transcendental calls? For CALL, computes erf(d1), erf(d2), exp(-rT), exp(-qT). For PUT additionally erf(-d1), erf(-d2) = 4 erf. But note cumulativeNormal(-d1) = 1 - cumulativeNormal(d1) mathematically, but not bit-identical and could lose precision for small values? Actually CDF complement via 1-N(d1) suffers cancellation for large d1 (tail). Original PUT uses cumulativeNormal(-d2) and cumulativeNormal(-d1), not 1-N. We should preserve semantics. Cannot substitute. But we could compute erf(-x) = -erf(x) exactly? IEEE erf is odd, so erf(-x) = -erf(x) exactly for glibc? std::erf(-x) returns exactly -std::erf(x) likely. CUDA too probably. Then cumulativeNormal(-d1) = 0.5*(1 - erf(d1*M_SQRT1_2)) = 1 - Nd1 exactly? Floating: 0.5*(1.0 - e) vs 1 - 0.5*(1.0+e). These are not always bit-identical due to rounding. But could compute both from erf values with fewer special calls: erf(d1), erf(d2), then for put use 0.5*(1-erf(d1...)) etc. That reduces 4 erf to 2 erf for puts, potentially ~25% faster overall (mix of calls/puts). But does it maintain equivalent semantics? Mathematically identical, but floating differs by ulps. We already differ from CPU; within tolerance. But careful: using `1 - Nd1` for Nd1 near 1 loses relative precision, but computing `0.5*(1 - erf)` keeps absolute precision. For put, cumulativeNormal(-d2) = 0.5*(1 + erf(-d2)) = 0.5*(1 - erf(d2)). This is accurate. So we can reuse e1=erf(d1*invSqrt2), e2=erf(d2*invSqrt2); Nd1=0.5*(1+e1), Nd2=0.5*(1+e2); call price uses Nd1,Nd2. For put, need cdf(-d2)=0.5*(1-e2), cdf(-d1)=0.5*(1-e1). This avoids 2 erf calls per put. For call, same 2. This is a legitimate algebraic optimization that preserves accuracy (actually more accurate than 1-N). It changes results at ulp level. Good for performance.\n\nCan we reduce exp calls? discount=exp(-rT) and dividendDiscount=exp(-qT). Both needed. For the test data r=q? In test options, r and q sometimes equal (for most, q=0.1, r=0.1), so exp(-rT)=exp(-qT); we could check if q==r and reuse (branch). But data-dependent branch might hurt; for the benchmark's data many are equal so beneficial.
